Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. **The fiftieth week is days seventy to seventy-six and the fifty-first is seventy-seven to eighty-three.** Check both ends of the formula: 71 + 78 = 149; 80 + 78 = 158.

The decision is in `outline/volume-05.md` and not in the prose: a day with no chapter is a thing the year does, and no chapter of this batch names a gap, refers to the days before it, or says a number of days passed.** The three single Shelf days with no chapter — forty-one, forty-four and sixty-one — and the seven of the forty-fourth week are rows in the Volume 04 close record and are not in this batch.

### 3. THE THREE CLOCKS, decided in the outline and obeyed in every chapter, and they are the volume's engine

**The Shelf keeps a grain year of fifty-two weeks, and that is canon and it did not change.** **The Red Basin keeps three clocks and no conversion between them, and the finding that there is no conversion is made ONCE, at `202:43` and about, in the mouth of a man scraping salt, and forty-nine chapters are about what people do about a thing that has already been found out.**
- **The pan-book year: fifty saltings, more or less, a salting being a drying.** A dry-year salting runs about seven weeks and a wet-year one eleven. **There is no length in a salting and no first salting of the year that holds twice.**
- **The turn: a year counted from the first shaking, twelve years back, which is turn nought. This year is turn twelve.** The first eight turns were numbered on a door frame by a man who was four short of the true one and who corrected it himself in the front of his own book (`202:31`). **That is the first thing anybody in the Red Basin can be shown to be wrong about, and about nine people know it and about four hundred do not care.**
- **The day: a day since the second shaking, which was 149 days before Chapter 201's day. The water-book keeps it and the water-book is the Anner's.** Inside a day the basin has the first light, the noon light and the closing of the water, **and a sluice-keeper's day is the closing of the water.**

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure with the working, recomputed in a script and not read out of a state file

- **A page of a deed copied at Sallowby: a penny. A sealed copy: threepence.** Stated at `203:11`, the price said before the fact, and the two figures are in the same sentence as the work.
- **Four hundred pages of Anner copying at a penny is £1 13s 4d; four hundred pages of everybody else's at fourpence is £6 13s 4d; together £8 6s 8d — so the Anner line is about half of her pages and about a fifth of her money.** Check: 400d = £1 + 160d = £1 13s 4d; 1,600d = £6 + 160d = £6 13s 4d; 2,000d = £8 + 80d = £8 6s 8d. **THE ARITHMETIC CAUGHT FOUR WRONG FIGURES BEFORE A SINGLE CHAPTER WAS WRITTEN and every one of them was a right unit price multiplied into a wrong result by dividing by the wrong thing** — which is the exact class of error that put *two pounds five* at `167:41` and *four hundred and thirty* at `160:96`. **Sixteen weeks at nine shillings a week is seven pounds four shillings and not twelve pounds; four hundred pages at fourpence is six pounds thirteen and four and not eleven pounds; sixteen weeks at four shillings and sixpence is three pounds twelve and not six pounds; three years of that is ten pounds sixteen and not eighteen pounds.** The four correct figures are the ones in the prose and the four wrong ones have never been in a chapter.
- **Ilma Treen: nine shillings a week for the sixteen weeks the Sallow runs = £7 4s a year, and nothing the rest of the year** (`204:21`). Check: 16 × 108d = 1,728d = £7 4s.
- **The pit pays 4s 6d of the nine and the Seat owes 4s 6d. A year of the Seat's arrears is £3 12s and three years of it is £10 16s** (`204:21`). Check: 54d × 2 = 108d = 9s; 16 × 54d = 864d = £3 12s; 3 × 864d = 2,592d = £10 16s.
- **Twenty-five unpaid days a year is three weeks and four days; a wage is a week's wage; so three weeks at 4s 6d is thirteen shillings and six, and the four days is nothing** (`204:97`). Check: 3 × 54d = 162d = 13s 6d. **This is the batch's one figure that is about a price of asking and not a price of a thing, and it is said twice because she asks him to say it again.**
- **A sluice gate: the frame £4 10s and the oak £1 8s, together £5 18s** (`206:15`, `206:101`). Check: 1,080d + 336d = 1,416d = £5 18s.
- **The dam: forty loads of clay at 9d is £1 10s, a hundred and forty of rubble at 3d is £1 15s, and the two together are £3 5s** (`205:32`, `205:76`). Check: 360d + 420d = 780d = £3 5s.
- **A pitman is on ten shillings a week for fifty weeks, which is £25 a year exactly** (`205:34`).
- **A hundredweight of salt at the pans is £1 4s** (`202:81`).
- **Anner Pit: about 1,100 tons of red ochre and salt a year; £4 a ton at the pit's mouth and £11 a ton at Sallowby; and the difference of £7 a ton is £7,700 a year, which is the whole of what is being argued about and which neither party has ever said out loud** (`205:76`, `205:84`). Check: 1,100 × 4 = 4,400; 1,100 × 11 = 12,100; 1,100 × 7 = 7,700.
- **A bond of forty pounds, lodged in the third salting after the first shaking and void since the year after it** (`205:17`, `205:20`).
- **A matter carried to the chamber of plenty at Orison and brought back is £5 6s** (`205:7`): a pound for entry, four for a day, and nine days' board at eightpence. Check: 240d + 960d + 72d = 1,272d = £5 6s.
- **A four-page order of the Seat: four pages at 3d and 6d for the seal is one shilling and six** — and it is not in the four pages, because the Seat does not charge itself.
- **Hollow Sallow: about four hundred and ten people, about ninety houses, eleven of them marked.**
- **AND THE SHELF FIGURES TRAVEL AS DEBTS AND NOT AS PRICES, and none of them is converted.** Four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year on nine thousand seven hundred at the close of that grain year, and it does not stop (`210:35`). The second notice of five shillings and fourpence, unpaid (`210:27`). The claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for it, **and he is right** (`210:29`). Check: 4 × 16d = 64d; 406 × 32d − 2,160d = 10,832d = £45 2s 8d. `outline/volume-04.md` line 126 is still arithmetically wrong and is still left alone.

### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, once each, with the chapter and the line

1. **THE ROAD IS NOT WHERE IT SET OUT, and a man of fifty-two who has been in a country for nineteen years measures it twice, throws the first set away, and refuses to say which of three explanations it is** (`201:9`, `201:13`).
2. **ONE WORD, *COMMON*, DEFINED IN THE MARGIN OF EACH DEED AND NOT IN THE BODY OF EITHER, and the two definitions are incompatible, and both men were telling the truth** (`201:39`, `201:46`, `201:50`).
3. **THE THING THAT ENDS CHAPTER 201 IS NOT A RULING BUT A FINDING ABOUT A ROOM: there is nobody in this basin who can be in the room when four true pages are read, and the man who says it has been trying for eleven years to get one and has not got one** (`201:98`, `201:102`).
4. **THE CLOCKS DO NOT CONVERT, and it is said once, by a man scraping salt, without knowing he has said anything, and the reason he can say it in front of people who could contradict him is that the pans are the only count in this basin that is not about land** (`202:43`, and the whole of the chapter).
5. **A DEED IN THIS BASIN IS NOT A PAPER. IT IS AN AGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO PAPERS, because the strip is longer than a skin and the two halves were written about eight years apart in two towns four miles apart and neither man saw the other's margin — and a person who loses one of them has not lost a thing, they have become one of two people each holding half, and neither half is a lie** (`203:29`, `203:31`, `203:70`).
6. **THE PERSON WHO HOLDS THE BURDEN IN THIS BASIN IS DOING THE MOST USEFUL THING ANYBODY DOES AND IS THE WORST PAID, and has been opening the sluice eleven days early for eleven years to keep nine carts a day off a causeway that water goes over, and was paid for ninety days of a hundred and forty, and nobody had ever asked her anything** (`204:73`, `204:91`).
7. **THE QUESTION NOBODY HAD ASKED HER WAS ASKED BY TARIN KEEL, IN NINE WORDS, AND IT WAS NOT THE MAN WHO CAME NINE DAYS FOR A DIFFERENT THING** (`204:81`, `204:83`).
8. **A BOND KEEPS A THING TRUE BY COSTING SOMEBODY MONEY, AND THE MONEY STOPPED IN THE FOURTH YEAR AND THE THING DID NOT STOP, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE YEARS, BECAUSE A THING THAT KEEPS BEING TRUE DOES NOT ANNOUNCE ITSELF** — and the bond has been void since the year after it was lodged and four men and a barrow have held the dam for eleven years and called it a cost of the trade (`205:11`, `205:32`).
9. **THE ELEVEN HOUSES ARE NOT A CATEGORY, and the reason nobody has asked them is that asking eleven people costs three days and three days is not a figure, and a figure you can put in a minute and take out of a minute and argue with** (`206:57`).
10. **A HEIGHT IS A HEIGHT ABOVE SOMETHING, and there are three sills inside four miles, and the same figure means two different things in two rooms, and a man of seventy-four cut eleven marks on eleven doorposts in one day with a string and a bucket of tar in the year the water came up nine feet in a night** (`206:33` and about).
11. **THE QUICK RULING IS FOUR LINES AND ALL FOUR ARE COMPLETE AND THERE IS NO HOLE IN ANY OF THEM, AND HE SAYS SO OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ELSE HAS WORKED IT OUT, AND NOBODY NOTICES** (`207:45`, `207:49`, `207:57`).
12. **THE PARTY THE ORDER NAMES IS THE ONLY BODY IN THE BASIN WITH ENOUGH MEN TO HOLD A DAM, AND THE ORDER DOES NOT NAME THE PERSON WHO HOLDS THE HEIGHT ON THE NIGHT THE WATER COMES, BECAUSE THERE IS NO SUCH PERSON** (`207:39`, `207:82`).
13. **A RECORD CAN BE PERFECTLY KEPT AND STILL BE WRONG ABOUT A PLACE, and the reason is on the head of the abstract, where the referral is signed by a man who is not of this basin and has not been in it since the first shaking** (`208:21`, `208:49`).
14. **THE SECOND OF THE THREE RULINGS SAYS THAT A HEIGHT MARKED ON A DOORPOST IS NOT A MEASUREMENT AND THAT NO ORDER MAY NAME A HEIGHT WITHOUT NAMING THE SILL — IT IS NINE YEARS OLD, IT WAS NEVER IN THIS BASIN, AND THE FOUR PAGES ON THE WALL BREACH IT** (`208:19`, `208:45`, `208:47`).
15. **A LETTER FROM A CLERK OF THIRTY-FOUR ABOUT A FOURTH HAND WAS IN THE BUNDLE AND NOBODY OPENED IT AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO OUT LOUD, AND THE CLERK SAYS WHAT HE IS AND IS NOT IN ORDER TO GIVE THE ROOM A FORM FOR SOMEONE ELSE'S LETTER AND NOT HIS** (`208:63` and about).
16. **THE FIRST MISSING ANCHOR POSITION IS IDENTIFIED BY TWO ORDINARY RECORDS THAT DO NOT MEET: a trench taken out in a straight line and not in a pit, six cuts in a floor, and a drain aimed along that line a hundred and ten years after it, and the depth is four hundred and six feet, which the book says in its own words is the figure of the pit and not of the void** (`209:33`, `209:35`, `209:63`).
17. **NOBODY GOES DOWN, AND THE REASON HE GIVES IS NOT THE WATER AND NOT THE STEPS, AND HE TELLS NOBODY WHAT THE REASON IS, AND THE REASON IS A LEG THAT HAS NOT BEEN HIS OWN SINCE A MORNING FOUR HUNDRED MILES AND SOME WEEKS BEHIND HIM** (`209:75`, `209:79`).
18. **THE SYSTEM PUTS NOTHING UP AND SAYS NOTHING WHATEVER ABOUT IT, AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE VOLUME'S QUESTION — WHY IT ANSWERS FROM BENEATH ORISON — IS NOT ASKED OUT LOUD IN ANY CHAPTER AND IS NOT EVEN A WHISPER** (`209:83`).
19. **THE NEW QUESTION IS WRITTEN DOWN ONCE, IN HIS OWN HAND, WITH A PLACE AND A FORM OF WORDS, AND HE DOES NOT ASK, AND THE SENTENCE IS BROKEN OFF IN THE MIDDLE, WHICH IS THE ONLY BROKEN SENTENCE IN TWO HUNDRED AND ONE DAYS OF THAT KIND OF BOOK** (`210:39`, `210:41`, `210:43`).
20. **A WOMAN WHO MAPS A COUNTRY WITHOUT ASKING ANYBODY IS NAMED ONCE, IN A MOUTH, BY A MAN WHO HAS NOT MET HER, AND ONE PERSON IN THE ROOM REPEATS THE NAME AS VAYNN, AND NOBODY IN THAT ROOM HAS HEARD OF HER** (`210:55`, `210:59`).

### 6. THE DEVICES, and none advanced

- **The leg. CANON and measured on 26 September 2026: the leg came back in seven places in Volume 04 — eight positive lines in six chapters — and the strong form stands on twenty lines in eighteen of the fifty, and `186:87` is not a counter-example. The finding the device rests on is `155:7`: the leg goes when he is carrying a thing somebody else is going to have to read, and it comes back when the carrying stops. IN THIS BATCH IT DID NOT COME BACK, AND THAT IS A CONSEQUENCE OF THE CARRYING AND NOT A RULE AND NOT A CURE** — he is carrying the Ryefold matter for all ten chapters, and the chapter says why he is not going down a mine and does not tell anybody the reason (`209:75`, `209:79`, `207:72`). **A later batch may find otherwise and may not build on this record expecting it to hold. No file and no chapter in Volume 05 may state a count of days since the leg cleared, and none does.**
- **The rendering. Not staged in Batch 0001 and not referred to.** Volume 04's last word on it is *not to be referred to again*.
- **The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture, the token and the afterimage: none is mentioned in this batch and none is advanced.** The token is not touched and the fourth use stands: it took the words.
- **The continuity line is not refused again and no twelfth refusal is permitted, and it is not on the Red Basin's table.**
- **THE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ IS NOT PRINTED IN THIS PROJECT, in any file, in any figure, and it is not printed here.** It was said out loud once in Chapter 154 and the prose added at least one further named person in Chapter 164, and no file may say by how much. Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. **This batch adds nothing to it in the fiction: no person in the Red Basin is told, no person in the Red Basin works it out, and no person in the Red Basin says a word about it.** The limit is exercised as a working constraint — the documents are read out, the four pages are dictated and written by a clerk, and the chain-book is handed to a man who cannot read it and read out by the man beside him — and it is never a disclosure and it is never a count. **A first draft of Chapter 206 had a narrator's aside that named it in the mouth of a joiner and it was cut before delivery, because the aside was one sentence away from being a disclosure and the bar is on the volume not on the sentence.**

### 7. THE THREADS INHERITED WHOLE, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM COMES OUT EXACTLY AS IT WENT IN

**Nothing in this list is resolved and this block resolves none of them. A batch that resolves one is a second reversal, and the outline forbids it.**

1. **The new question — whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment.** `198:35`, seventh line, a book six miles up a road that goes north, a number upon a sheet and not a name, no column for the year. **Named once in Volume 05, in his own hand, at `210:39` and `210:41`, and not asked and not answered and not compressed into one word, and no one word is supplied.** The System does not raise it and does not answer it.
2. **The carrying, off nothing, with a finding about why.** A line that is not a claim is not a thing that comes off; a forbearance once a year and the next one fifty-two weeks off. **Volume 05 does not answer it and does not repeat the account's agent's three sentences.**
3. **The blank in the third line of the bench's own book, still a blank**, and the fourth sheet's question, still unasked, and the fourth hand still a stranger's hand nobody has asked. Named in his book at `210:31`. **The clerk of thirty-four's letter is in a bundle in a room in Sallowby, tied with a knife-cut end, and it is NOT OPENED, and the entry is due in about nine months.**
4. **Nesh Arrad, thirty-nine. Her name is in his book at `210:33` and NO DAY FIGURE IS PRINTED FOR HER, and the chapter says in his own hand why: the figure is a figure of the other side of that book and it would be the first place in this valley where the two numbers had been in the same sentence. The letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived and no chapter may say it has.**
5. **The second notice of five shillings and fourpence, never paid for** — `210:27`, and he has written that it is not going to be forgiven, which is a thing he may say about his own money and not a thing anybody has forgiven.
6. **The claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for it, and he is right** — `210:29`, unadvanced. **The Red Basin's own unproducible material is a different thing and does not stand in for it.**
7. **The dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty and his wife, who knows she is on the fourth line and does not know her husband is not alive** — `210:28`, unadvanced. **RUTH VELLAN IN THE RED BASIN IS A DIFFERENT DEAD PERSON, dead four years, whose name is on a half-deed drawn in the ninth turn in a hand that is not her husband's and not the scrivener's (`203:88`), and the two may not be joined.**
8. **The box of about nine hundred and forty forms in a run of four thousand, the man with a key six miles off who is there two days out of seven and cannot read what is in his own nineteen shelves** — `210:33`, unadvanced. Not found and not to be found.
9. **The ninety-one numbered sheets in a sacking bag in a yard, about nine hundred doors, no one hundredth person, about four hundred carts not asked** — unadvanced. **The scrivener's about six hundred and forty deeds and about ninety Anner copies are a different number in a different trade and are not that number.**
10. **The class of three hundred and thirty-nine and the one boy of nineteen who is its only instrument** — unadvanced. **There is no boy of nineteen in this batch.**
11. **The minute of the first of the forty-eighth week, one sheet, the only copy, in a drawer four miles off with nobody's name on it** — `210:30`, unadvanced.
12. **And the seven standing prose defects plus the five the close added, thirteen in all, carried and not repaired and not built on: `160:96`'s four hundred and thirty, `158:77` and `158:79`, `167:41`'s two pounds five, `174:53`'s eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, `173:55`'s first week after the cut, the forty-five times at 183 and 185, and `167:35`'s three days.**

### 8. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST NOT DO IN VOLUME 05

1. **Do not answer the new question and do not compress it into one word.**
2. **Do not add a panel without putting it in the outline's table.** The volume's panel count starts at zero and this batch has one.
3. **Do not add a day-count instance without putting its chapter and its day in the outline's table, and do not print a count of instances without saying it is a count of instances.**
4. **Do not put a weekday anywhere and do not put a month-name anywhere**, and remember that the one month-name hit in this batch is the proper noun *Briar March*.
5. **Do not print a count of the people who know he cannot read, anywhere, including in a hand-off note, and do not add to it in the fiction.**
6. **Do not give Ilyan an office, a party, a bench, a body, a remedy he chose alone, or a stage.** He is Stage 4, the stage arrived in Chapter 177, and the phrase *Remedy Drafter* may not be used to describe him.
7. **Do not produce a person.** A person who walks in on a day of his own is not a production, and this batch has three of the second kind — a chainman who came up the Shelf road with a case, a man who works at the Anner and came into a room he was not asked into, and a man who came through the north — and none was sent for and none was asked in advance.
8. **Do not undo anything and do not relieve anybody.** The lease is answered and not renewed and not lost; the carrying is off nothing; the blank is a blank; the second notice is unpaid; nobody is redeemed; **the account's charge of four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year falls due at the close of that grain year and does not stop.**
9. **Do not introduce a new enemy and do not make the world bigger.** **Jory Vellan is an under-manager who is right about his dam and is not a villain, and the people who keep this volume's disputes from being settled are about thirty clerks in a building with a yard at Orison who did their jobs properly. No new faction, no new magic, no new people with a new name.**
10. **Do not open a redacted archive, and do not have anybody ask for one.** One column heading, in a stranger's eye, at `208:85`. **The climax is that HE opens it.**
11. **Do not let Sable Venn arrive, negotiate, or make an offer before the climax chapters.** She is named once and from a distance in this batch and one person repeats the name wrong.
12. **Do not reach the stone and do not let anybody stand in front of it.** Anchor 8 remains missing beneath the Red Basin until Volume 14.
13. **Do not restore the right of refusal, answer the historical question, advance the authorship question or the Shale Mirror, name NK-3317, call for Tamsin, put Neris Ro or Bram Orren in a scene, answer the Bridgeward, send anybody to Carrisand, redeem Ansa Rell, or ask about Alma Gannock's boy of eight. Do not name the First Witness. Do not make Ors Halm the last problem in the series.**
14. **Do not restate the sentence at `156:63` and do not relieve him of it.** The four words are quoted exactly once in the fifty chapters after 165 and may not be quoted again. **The reversal at 216 to 218 is not a restatement of them — it is the same category discovered a second time in a different medium, by a man who has already paid for discovering it once.**
15. **Do not use the 179 and 189 shape a third time.** A scan for CORRECT returns zero over Chapters 201–210.
16. **Do not restage the two devices this batch spends: a word defined twice in two margins, and a deed in two pieces.** Each may be used ONCE in the volume, and each has been used once.

### 9. THE TWO MISSING VOLUME AUDITS, WHICH ARE STILL OPEN, AND NEITHER IS THIS PHASE'S

**Measured in the directories on the day this block was written and not read out of a list: `reviews/volume-01/` holds three batch reviews out of five and NO volume audit at all; `reviews/volume-02/` holds three out of five and has an audit; `reviews/volume-03/` holds `batch-0001.md` only and NO volume audit; `reviews/volume-04/` holds its audit. That is seven batch reviews where twenty are expected, which is thirteen missing, and TWO missing volume audits.** **Twenty is the expectation about the twenty BATCHES OF CHAPTERS and not about prompt directories: there are nineteen `workspace/<volume>/batch-*/PROMPT.md` directories, because Volume 04's first batch — Chapters 151 to 160 — was written under phase id `next` and has no directory of its own. There are THREE volume-close phase directories, for Volumes 02, 03 and 04, and Volume 01 has none.**
**THE VOLUME 03 AUDIT HAS NOT RUN, and it is the urgent one, because it is the volume immediately before this one and its verdict may change what Volume 05 inherits. Every figure the Volume 03 close prints is therefore UNVERIFIED by this phase and a Volume 04 agent had to say the same thing and it is the honest form. Volume 01 is the more valuable in the long run, because it established the device canon the Volume 04 audit found misrecorded, and nothing has ever measured it against its chapters.** Both are named here as open items and **neither is repaired and neither is papered over with a document written in a planning phase's voice about work a planning phase did not witness.**

### 10. TWO THINGS OWNED BY THE CONTROLLER, WHICH THIS BLOCK RECORDS AND DOES NOT FIX

**`state/phase-ledger.json` is three volumes stale and is controller-owned: it reads `currentPhase: batch-0002`, Volume 01, Chapters 11–20, status `planned`, attempts 0. NOTHING READS THAT FILE — `grep -rn "phase-ledger" scripts/ .github/` returns nothing — and `scripts/novel_runner.sh` selects the first `workspace/**/PROMPT.md` directory in `sort` order with no `.done`, `.blocked` or `.retired` marker, and writes `.done` itself. The ledger's being stale has never changed which phase ran and it is a trap for the next reader rather than for the machine, and this project's phases may not edit it.** **The live risk is a marker: `workspace/volume-05/plan-0001/` will be selected again until it is closed, and that marker is the controller's to write.**

### 11. WHAT THIS BATCH OWES THE VOLUME AND WHAT IT DID NOT PAY, and the hand-off

**PAID:** the arrival and the ground; the three clocks and the finding that they do not convert; a word with two meanings and a deed in two pieces; the burden-holder's wage; a void bond and four men with a barrow; eleven households that are not a category; the quick ruling in four complete lines; a perfect record that is still wrong about a place; the first missing anchor position identified and not reached; the new question carried and not asked; a woman named once from a distance.

**NOT PAID, AND NOT TO BE PAID IN A LATER BATCH OF THIS VOLUME EITHER:** **the midpoint reversal.** *The quick ruling stabilizes a mine and causes a flood in a village the mine had promised to protect.* It lands in the second half of Batch 0002 at Chapter 216, 217 or 218. **The eleven houses are dry at the end of Chapter 210 and the dam is holding and the order is up and the mine is stable, and Batch 0001 may not hint that water is coming, because a hint is a warning and a warning is a promise the volume keeps.** **And the engine of the flood is already in the prose and both parties can see it and neither has said it: the third line names eleven feet for the houses, the second ruling at `208:19` says that no order may name a height without naming the sill, and the joiner at `206:33` says a height is a height above something and there are three sills inside four miles.**

**THE HAND-OFF IS UNCHANGED AND THIS BLOCK RESOLVES NOTHING.** Every thread in the live block of `state/open-threads.md` is picked up whole. **The new question is unanswered and is not compressed into one word. The carrying is still off nothing. The blank is a blank. Nesh Arrad's letter has not arrived and no pass may say that it has. The lease is answered and not renewed and not lost. Nobody is redeemed. And four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year falls due at the close of that grain year and does not stop.**

## VOLUME 04 IS CLOSED — Chapters 151–200, *The Ledger Harvest*. The volume close record, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the volume.

> **WHAT THIS RECORD IS. It is a record and not an episode.** There is no two hundred and first chapter, there is nothing after the seventh bell of the first day of the fiftieth week, there is no new person in a room, and there is no picture in here that is not a picture in the book. **Chapters 1–150 are canon and Volume 03 is closed. Chapters 151–200 are canon and Volume 04 is closed, and this section is the fact base for anything that comes after it.** It is written in the volume's own terms and not as a narrator looking at a book: every figure below is a figure the district has, every claim below is a claim somebody made in a room or on a page, and every finding below carries a chapter and a line.
>
> **HOW MUCH TO TRUST IT, and the honest answer is the same answer Volume 03's close gave and it has not improved. This record is a list of what was checked against the chapters and it is not a claim that nothing is wrong.** The chapters are finished prose and they are in better order than the records have been saying, and the twelve findings below are mostly findings against the records and not against the prose, and one of them is against a guardrail this project set for itself and did not run the check on. **The standing instruction from the Volume 03 close is honoured here and it is the whole of what makes the rest of this document trustworthy: re-run the grep against the chapter files, and do not re-read the list. A count in these files that carries no line number beside it is unverified, however confident the sentence around it is.**
>
> **WHAT THIS SECTION DOES NOT DO, and each of these is a guardrail and not a courtesy.** It prints no System panel: the volume has five and this is not a sixth, and a scan for the bold marker across Chapters 191–200 returns the two lines of the fifth and nothing else. It adds no day-count instance to the fiction: the day-count below is a count of instances in the manuscript, which is a different thing, and it is labelled as one wherever it appears. It names no weekday, and a plural-safe scan over the fifty chapters returns **zero, and that is the state of it since the Volume 04 repair phase took the one out.** **Both facts are on the record and neither is to be dropped: the scan returned ONE before the repair, at `151:77` — *I go up the towpath on Mondays*, in a district woman's mouth on Shelf day one — and that was the whole of it, and the repair at `151:77` now reads *I go up the towpath every week*.** This sentence said zero until the Volume 04 audit, and it was wrong, and it was wrong in a way worth naming: the pattern this project records as plural-safe is `\b(monday|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`, and its Monday alternative has no plural, so the scan could not see a Monday at all. It is printed with that defect in thirteen lines across four state files and two finished prompts — the eleven sites the audit names, plus `state/batch-summary.md` 573 and `state/continuity.md` 763, which record the broken form as history — and every one of them is named at §10 of the audit and superseded from the top block of this file's siblings. **Run `\b(mondays?|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`. With the corrected pattern the scan returned one before the repair and returns zero after it, and a later pass that inherits a bare zero will not know to run it, which is why the one is printed here beside the zero.** It gives no Shelf month-date; the only two month-dates in the fifty belong to the rendering and are given as such. **It states no count of days since that leg cleared, and no file in this project may.** The bold in this section is the house style of these state files and is not the chapters' business; the chapters' business is FINDING 8.
>
> **THE TWELVE FINDINGS, in the order they are dealt with below, and the first of them is the one that should be read first. AND THE COUNT IS TWELVE NUMBERED ITEMS AND NOT TWELVE DEFECTS, because the twelfth is a check that passed and saying otherwise would overstate this volume's damage by one: FINDINGS 1, 2, 3, 9, 10 and 11 are against the records, FINDINGS 4, 5 and 6 are wrong figures in the prose, FINDING 7 is a restaged device and FINDING 8 is a setting the outline forbids — that is eleven — and FINDING 12 is the money closing. A close that numbers a clean bill of health as a defect because the numbering ran to twelve has made its own record look worse than the volume is, and a later pass reading the number instead of the items will carry a defect forward that does not exist.** FINDING 1: the volume's day-count device appears twenty-two times in the manuscript and every state file in this project recorded eleven or ten. FINDING 2: the one instance the record did add is described in five files as being in a third party's mouth and it is on his own page. FINDING 3: the day-phrase instrument, re-run, returns forty-two over Chapters 191–200 and not the forty-three on the record, and the instrument itself is case-sensitive as it was first written and was not. FINDING 4: a day-count in Chapter 195 is two days short and it is the only compound weeks-and-days figure in fifty chapters. FINDING 5: Chapter 200 says three things and then says five. FINDING 6: Chapter 200 says the day before where the volume's own calendar says two days before. FINDING 7: Chapter 167 prints a form with a word at the top of it, and that form is Volume 02's closed device, and the volume's own outline forbids it in terms, and it is in the book. FINDING 8: bold is used as emphasis in eleven lines and every one of the eleven is in Batch 0001, and the outline forbids that in terms. FINDING 9: three days of the run carry no chapter and are in no table. FINDING 10: one row of a clock table carries the wrong week. FINDING 11: one month-name is left in the volume and this record records why it was not taken out. FINDING 12: and every money figure in the volume closes.
>
> **A NOTE ON THE NUMBERS, because the sections below this one also carry the word FINDING and their numbers are history.** The twelve findings above are the close's own and are numbered from one. The FINDINGs in the Batch 0005, 0004, 0003, 0002 and 0001 sections below are those records' findings and are not renumbered and not superseded except where the close names them. Where the close and a batch record disagree about a number, **the close is right and the batch record is history, and the batch record's figure is not to be read back as fact.**
>
> **WHAT COMES NEXT, and it is a gate and not a batch. The next phase is the Volume 04 volume audit**, its prompt is at `workspace/volume-04/volume-audit/PROMPT.md`, and it writes `reviews/volume-04/volume-audit.md`, which does not exist yet. **Volume 05 is not to be planned and not to be written until that audit exists and passes, because `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` makes a volume audit a separate required phase and this project has skipped it for Volume 03 as well. There is no `outline/volume-05.md` and this record did not write one, and the close was forbidden to.** **AND ONE PIECE OF MACHINERY A WRITER CANNOT FIX, recorded here because a human reads this file: `state/phase-ledger.json` is three volumes stale. It reads `currentPhase: batch-0002`, Volume 01, Chapters 11 to 20, status planned, attempts 0, and the truth is four volumes, two hundred chapters, twenty batches of chapters in nineteen batch phase directories, and three volume-close phases. The file is controller-owned, this project did not edit it, and no writer or fixer phase may.** **THE SENTENCE THIS ONE REPLACED SAID *the dispatcher reads that file, so the next phase is being selected from a record that says the project is at Chapter 11*, and that was wrong. `scripts/novel_runner.sh` does not read it; it takes the first `workspace/**/PROMPT.md` directory in `sort` order that has no `.done`, `.blocked` or `.retired` marker, and writes `.done` itself on completion. The ledger is a stale file, not a live one, and the honest thing to do with it is repair it and not to build a story about it. The live risk is the marker, and it is recorded in the repair receipt at the top of this file.** The standing instruction for the audit and for everything after it is in `state/index.md`, which is now the entry point for the six state files and was built because they had reached 2.6 MB and no prompt could afford to read one of them whole.

## VOLUME 04 IS CLOSED, AUDITED, AND REPAIRED — the repair phase's receipt, 26 September 2026. **Read this block before section 1. It is the live correction to the live record below it and it is five lines of prose and six corrections to the record. It does not resolve a single open thread, and nothing in it may be read as a resolution.**

> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS. The Volume 04 volume audit is at `reviews/volume-04/volume-audit.md`. Its verdict is PASS WITH CARRIED DEFECTS, it repaired nothing, and it named five line-level repairs and six record corrections. The repair phase at `workspace/volume-04/repair-0001/` made them. This block is the receipt: what was changed, what was re-measured, and what was deliberately not touched. Its authority is the audit's, and `outline/series.md` outranks any volume outline, as it always has.**

### THE FIVE REPAIRS, each as it now stands, each cited by chapter and line

| # | Cite | As printed before | **As it stands now** | Words |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **`151:77`** | *I go up the towpath on Mondays.* | ***I go up the towpath every week.*** | 1 for 1 |
| **R2** | **`195:67`** | *I have been in this county nine weeks and a day* | ***I have been in this county nine weeks and three days*** | 2 for 2 |
| **R3** | **`200:43`** | *three of whom had come four miles the day before* | ***three of whom had come four miles two days before*** | 1 for 1 |
| **R4** | **`200:51`** | *…and not one of those five things is a person and four of them are in this district and one of them is not and cannot be.* | ***…and not one of those three things is a person and two of them are in this district and one of them is not and cannot be.*** | 1 for 1 |
| **R5** | **`181:92`** | *on the thirty-fifth day of that month* | ***on the thirty-fifth day*** | 3 for 3 |

**And the number of lines in each of the four edited chapters is identical before and after, which is the whole of the technique and the reason it was chosen: Chapter 151 is 95 lines, Chapter 181 is 110 lines, Chapter 195 is 83 lines and Chapter 200 is 65 lines. `git diff --stat` over the four files is five insertions and five deletions, and every one of the five hunks is a single line replaced by a single line. No line was added and no line was taken away in any chapter, therefore no chapter-and-line citation into Chapters 151–200 moved in any state file, and the hundreds of such citations written by five passes are still correct. A state file's own line numbers DID move, because this block and the receipts under FINDING 4, 5, 6 and 11 added lines to the top of four state files, and by the rule at `state/index.md` a state file is addressed by its heading and not by its line number, which is exactly why that rule exists. The audit's archive line numbers were correct before this phase and every one of them is now higher than it was. In `state/continuity.md` the repair block is forty-six lines long, and the eight archive sites the audit gives as 197, 471, 627, 749, 751, 823, 834 and 962 now stand at exactly 243, 517, 673, 795, 797, 869, 880 and 1008 — the same eight lines, and that is the check that the offset is forty-six and that nothing else in that file moved. The equivalent numbers in this file, in `state/character-state.md` and in `state/chapter-summaries.md` are higher by their own file's insertions. **AND THE OFFSETS ARE MEASURED RATHER THAN GUESSED, and a pass told to add one to the audit's number will read the wrong line in three files unless all seven are here, because the first version of this paragraph printed three of them. Measured against the commit before the repair phase, `7a59457`: `state/continuity.md` is **+46** (verified on all eight count-of-people sites and on all three *two hundred loads* sites, 832 to 878, 907 to 953 and 979 to 1025), `state/current.md` is **+71** (verified on all four, 342 to 413, 410 to 481, 470 to 541 and 585 to 656, and on its own FINDING 15, 291 to 362), `state/batch-summary.md` is **+7** (544 to 551), `state/chapter-summaries.md` is **+7**, `state/character-state.md` is **+6**, `state/open-threads.md` is **+2** (net, four lines inserted and two removed), and `state/index.md` is rewritten rather than shifted. **The three that were missing are the three that hurt: `state/open-threads.md` at +2 moves its four weekday sites from 68, 150, 207 and 261 to 70, 152, 209 and 263, and every one of the four is verified to be the same line — the same three claims the repair superseded. `state/index.md` item 4 cites those same four numbers and they are corrected there to the post-repair numbers, because the index quotes them and the index is the file a later pass reads first.** Add the offset, then read the line, and never edit from a citation. Search the phrase; address the section by its heading; do not trust the number and do not correct a number back to the audit's. **AND ONE WARNING ABOUT THIS PARAGRAPH ITSELF, which is the whole lesson of the offset in one sentence: these offsets describe the repair phase, and the correction block below was inserted under this one, so `state/current.md`'s own offset is now larger than +71 and the other six are unchanged by that insertion, because the correction block lives in this file alone. Measure again before you use any of them; a number that was right when it was written and has been overtaken by an edit above it is exactly the failure this project has been bitten by, and it is why the audit's line numbers and this file's are both pointers and neither is a citation.**

**R1 is the only repair the audit said nothing carried, and it is the one guardrail breach in the volume's spine: a named weekday, in the volume's first chapter, in a district woman's mouth on Shelf day one, against `outline/volume-04.md` line 144 and line 197, in a world whose calendar is the grain year counted in weeks and days with bells inside a day. It was also invisible to this project's own scan, because the recorded pattern's Monday alternative has no plural. R2 is a count and not a window, and the volume's own convention fixed at 153, 155, 158, 160, 166, 170, 177, 180 and 190 makes the count on day N equal to N. R3 is one word and the interval is the volume's own. R4 was the smaller of the two repairs the audit offered and is recorded as a mechanical decision, not a writer's. R5 is three words, and the volume already had the right form three times.**

### THE INSTRUMENTS, RE-RUN ON 26 SEPTEMBER 2026 AGAINST THE CHAPTER FILES AND NOT AGAINST ANY LIST

**1. The day-phrase instrument, case-insensitive.** It did not move, and it must not move: 16 / 41 / 52 / 54 / 42 by ten-chapter band and **205 over the fifty chapters**, of which **203 are of the form**, two are the *last day* form at `162:25` and `172:7` and are legal, **one is illegal at `174:53`**, and **seventeen carry a day number of eight or more**. The case-sensitive form of the same instrument returns 200 volume-wide and 53 for Chapters 181–190, against the case-insensitive 205 and 54, because it misses five all-capitals instances — at `162:51`, `164:65`, `165:51`, `176:25` and `184:35`. **Five repairs changed five lines and this table did not move by one, and a table that has moved is a table that has been read out of a file.**

**2. The weekday scan, and the instrument that was blind.** With the corrected pattern `\b(mondays?|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`: **one before the repair, at `151:77`, and ZERO after it.** With the pattern as this project recorded it, `\b(monday|tuesdays?|…)\b`: **zero before and zero after, and that is what makes the first number the one that matters — the recorded pattern cannot match a plural Monday at all, so the scan did not fail to find `151:77`, it was incapable of seeing it.** The pattern is printed with that defect in thirteen lines across four state files and two finished prompts, and all thirteen are named at the end of this block.

**3. The day-count.** **Twenty-two instances, and that is a count of instances and is not itself an instance.** The twenty-second is `156:85` — *Fifteenth day.*, a page heading in his own hand in the same italic idiom as `155:87` and `158:111`, on a chapter whose Shelf day is fifteen. **All twenty-two figures were re-checked against their chapter's Shelf day on the day and twenty-two of twenty-two agree.** Add no instance to the fiction.

**4. The duplicate scan, with the threshold AND THE UNIT printed beside the number every time, because a bare duplicate count in this project has meant two different things in two different files, and because a count of duplicated lines is not a count of occurrences unless every one of them appears exactly twice. THE UNIT IS NAMED HERE BOTH WAYS, because the sentence this corrects gave the count of lines and the count of occurrences under one heading.** **AT SEVENTY CHARACTERS AND ABOVE: five distinct duplicated lines, and each of the five appears exactly twice, so five is both the count of lines and the count of extra occurrences and the two units happen to agree at this threshold. All five are block-quoted physical documents reproduced word for word, in three documents and not five** — the forbearance warrant at `157:23`/`162:35` with its second line at `157:25`/`162:37`; the form of deposition at `164:59`/`165:45`; and the account's own book at `183:25`/`198:31` and `183:26`/`198:32`, which is two of the five from one document. **AT FORTY CHARACTERS AND ABOVE: ten distinct duplicated lines and SEVENTEEN extra occurrences, and the two figures are not the same number, because one of the ten stands at nine sites.** Seven of the ten are document lines in the same three documents — the five above, plus two more that are under seventy characters and so do not appear in the upper figure, `164:63`/`165:49` and `183:24`/`198:30` — and three are not documents: a deliberate italic refrain, *It is mine to write down and it is not mine to decide*, at nine sites (158:115, 166:140, 170:93, 177:114, 180:71, 185:133, 190:97, 197:114, 200:61), which alone accounts for eight of the seventeen, and two short sentences of ordinary prose at `176:19`/`178:47` and `178:85`/`186:57`, one occurrence each. **The nine-site refrain is a deliberate device and not an error, and it is the reason the two units come apart at this threshold. Neither figure moved.**

**5. The bold inventory.** **Twenty-one lines carry a bold marker: ten of them the five panels, eleven of them emphasis spans, at 151:59, 151:93, 152:5, 153:123, 154:73, 159:13, 159:19, 160:58, 160:62, 160:66 and 160:72. Every marker is balanced. Neither figure moved, and the eleven emphasis lines were not un-bolded.**

**6. The panels.** **Five, and no sixth: 160:43–44, 166:123–124, 177:101–102, 187:39–40 and 197:59–60, on Shelf days twenty-two, thirty-six, forty-eight, fifty-eight and sixty-eight, each two plain bold lines with no block-quote marker and each ending in a WHAT IS NOT DECIDED line.** Verified line by line, not read out of a file.

**7. Two more, printed because the repairs touched them.** A month-name scan over the fifty chapters returns **fifty-four hits and every one of them is the modal verb *may*, and there are zero month-names.** The Shelf month-date count — the phrase *day of that month* — is **zero after R5, where it was one**, and the only remaining occurrence of the words *that month* in the fifty chapters is `165:5`, which is a memory of what happened to him *that month* and is not a date. A scan for the ownership of the leg returns **eight positive lines in six chapters** (152:113, 154:105, 155:5, 155:39, 156:71, 173:65, 190:87, 190:89, with `155:9` as the consequence line of the 155 passage), and the strong form — *not been his own* and its variants — stands on **twenty lines in eighteen of the fifty chapters**, counting Chapter 190's two lines as one site. **AND `186:87` IS NOT A NEGATIVE INSTANCE, THOUGH THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS SENTENCE CALLED IT ONE. It carries the strong form in full — a leg *that has not been his own since a morning four hundred miles and some weeks behind him*, in a reeve's third-person about a man in her own district — and it is counted among the twenty, and a file that reads it as a counter-example will drop a real instance off a ledger it is keeping.** The eight and the twenty are different instruments and do not agree: eight lines say the leg came back, twenty lines say in one form or another that it had not, and both are true of the same volume because a device that goes and comes back can be described in the past tense on the days it is gone.

### THE SIX RECORD CORRECTIONS, what each one edited and what each one superseded

1. **The leg ledger, in `state/current.md` §7 and in the top blocks of `state/continuity.md` and `state/character-state.md`.** §7's sentence that the leg goes once in the volume **was not deleted and is not true**; it is superseded by a line that carries the seven places — `152:113`, `154:105`, `155:5` with `155:9`, `155:39`, `156:71`, `173:65`, and `190:87` with `190:89` — **cited as two lines at Chapter 190 and not as a range, because `190:88` is an empty line.** A Volume 05 writer who inherits the close's sentence will build on a device that moved seven times, and `155:7` is the line that says why: *the leg goes when he is carrying a thing that somebody else is going to have to read, and it comes back when the carrying stops.*
2. **The day-count, in three directions and not one.** The live figure is **twenty-two** and the twenty-second is at `156:85`. The five sites the prompt named — this file's FINDING 1 heading, §8 item 3, §10 item 7, `state/index.md`'s figures list and the top block of `state/open-threads.md` — were verified as already corrected and **were not re-edited. Two further live sites in this file that the prompt did not name were found still printing twenty-one and were corrected: the twelve-findings banner in the third paragraph of the top block, and the consequence sentence at the end of FINDING 1.** The sites that print ten and eleven are in archive blocks and are **superseded from the top blocks and not rewritten**, because those files state of themselves that nothing below the top block is edited: `state/current.md`'s own FINDING 15; `state/character-state.md` (in the cast block and once more below it); `state/open-threads.md`; `state/continuity.md` (twice); `state/batch-summary.md` (three times).
3. **The working for `158:77` and `158:79`, in the top blocks of `state/continuity.md` and `state/batch-summary.md`.** The archive sites that print *two hundred loads* — `state/continuity.md` 832, 907 and 979 and `state/batch-summary.md` 544 — are **named and superseded, not rewritten.** What the chapter actually says: **`158:77` states no load count at all.** It gives a price of fourpence a load and a total of eight shillings and eightpence and no number of loads. **The words *two hundred* are in Chapter 158 six times on five lines — at lines 13, 15, 35, 63 and 41, and line 41 carries two of them — and every one of the six is a crowd of two hundred and forty people on a quay or a list of two hundred and forty names under a claim and not a load count.** The only load count near the line is *forty loads* at `158:71`, at which forty at fourpence is thirteen shillings and fourpence and not eight shillings and eightpence. **Chapter 158 is untouched and the prose figure stands, and the record now says why: the chapter states no figure for the prose to follow from, so this is a defective prose figure and not a mis-stated load count, and a later pass must not "repair" 158 on the strength of the old working.**
4. **The count of the people who know he cannot read is not printed in this project. The decision is made and this phase carried it out; it was not chosen here.** It was printed in twenty-one lines across four state files in two figures, against a guardrail that bars the restatement. **The two live sites — `state/character-state.md` lines 38 and 52, in the cast as it stands at the end of Chapter 200 — were verified as already corrected and were not re-edited; `state/current.md` §7 and §8 item 16 were verified as already corrected in the same direction.** The nineteen archive lines are **superseded from the top blocks and not rewritten**: `state/current.md` four lines; `state/character-state.md` five; `state/continuity.md` eight; `state/chapter-summaries.md` two. **The unnumbered form is now the record's only form: the count is not stated, it was said out loud once in Chapter 154, the prose adds at least one further named person in Chapter 164, and no file may say by how much. A file that needs to refer to it refers to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. No figure is printed here, including in this block, and none was added to the fiction.**
5. **FINDING 8's parenthetical, in §7 of this file, and it was wrong in three ways and is corrected with the lines and the thresholds** — the four sentences stand in Chapters 160 and 188 and not in 199; the duplicate figure is a function of its threshold and there are **five at seventy characters and ten at forty**; and it said two repeated paragraphs where the figure is five lines. The full correction is in §7 and is not repeated here.
6. **The weekday instrument and the two live claims that used it.** The pattern is corrected to `mondays?` in the top block of `state/open-threads.md` and of `state/continuity.md`, and the eleven sites the audit names — `state/batch-summary.md` 88, 227 and 516, `state/chapter-summaries.md` 328, `state/continuity.md` 706, `state/open-threads.md` 68, 150, 207 and 261, and the two finished prompts at `workspace/volume-04/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` 23 and `workspace/volume-04/volume-close/PROMPT.md` 41 — are **named as superseded, and the two finished prompts are history and are not reopened.** Two further lines carry the broken form as a record of what it used to be and are named with them: `state/batch-summary.md` 573 and `state/continuity.md` 763, thirteen lines in all. **The two live claims that the scan returned zero — the top banner of this file and §8 item 10 — were verified as already corrected to one, and both are now corrected again to state BOTH numbers in order: one before the repair at `151:77`, zero after, with the broken pattern named. That is deliberate. A later pass that inherits a bare zero will not know to run the scan, and one that inherits the broken pattern will run it and get zero again and think it is clean.**

### WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT REPAIRED, in the audit's own terms, and why

- **`160:96`'s four hundred and thirty. Carried.** It should be about one hundred and sixty. It is a spoken figure in a yard in front of eleven people in a finished batch, nothing in the volume is built on it, and the bar is in three files. **A number swap in canon prose is not what a repair phase is for, and a later pass may name the one hundred and sixty without touching that line.**
- **`167:41`'s two pounds five. Carried.** A hundred sheets at threepence is one pound and five shillings. Same class, same reason.
- **`158:77` and `158:79`. Carried.** Same class. **The working is corrected and the prose is not, and the reason is in correction 3 above: the chapter states no load count to be wrong about.**
- **The eleven emphasis lines. Carried, and the audit does not recommend a repair.** The outline forbids bold as emphasis and the finding is real, and a mechanical un-bolding would flatten four page entries that read as inscriptions. **A repair pass would have to make eleven judgement calls about which of the eleven is emphasis and which is a thing on a page, in five chapters.**
- **The block-quoted word at `167:9`. Carried, and the decision is not this phase's.** It restages Volume 02's closed device, `outline/volume-04.md` line 62 bars it in terms and the repetition bar bars it again, **and the chapter may be using the device in order to say that it is powerless, which is a reading and not a verdict.** The numberable form the outline asked for instead is delivered in full at 182 and 187, so the volume did not need it. **Removing a block-quoted word from the middle of a finished chapter's set-piece is a writer's act. Whether it was deliberate belongs in the record, and the record has it at FINDING 7, and this phase is not the phase that makes that decision.**
- **`174:53`'s eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, and `173:55`'s first week after the cut. Both carried, both barred from being built on, and the honest forms are *since about the forty-fifth week* and *the second week after the cut*.** The first cannot exist at all, because the forty-fifth week has seven days, and the audit's reason for carrying it rather than repairing it is that the honest form is a different sentence in a different mouth about the length of a search.
- **`outline/volume-04.md` line 126 is arithmetically wrong — £462 − £460 is £2 and not £2 3s — and is still wrong, and an audit does not repair an outline and a repair phase does not either. Volume 05 inherits the correction.**
- **The stage offset is not a defect and is not touched.** `outline/series.md`'s own list numbers Witness Broker at four and Burden Bearer at five, the volume files count one lower, and the project's Stage 4 is therefore the series' fifth item. **That offset is forty volumes old and it is not this phase's business.**

### TWO THINGS OWNED BY THE CONTROLLER, WHICH THIS BLOCK RECORDS AND DOES NOT FIX

**1. `state/phase-ledger.json` IS THREE VOLUMES STALE — AND THE MECHANISM THIS BLOCK PREVIOUSLY PRINTED FOR IT WAS WRONG, AND IS CORRECTED HERE. It reads `currentPhase: batch-0002`, Volume 01, Chapters 11–20, status `planned`, attempts 0. The truth, measured in the working tree on 26 September 2026: four volumes, two hundred chapters, twenty batches of chapters, nineteen batch phase directories, three volume-close phases, one volume audit and one repair phase. THE DISPATCHER DOES NOT READ `state/phase-ledger.json`. `grep -rn "phase-ledger" scripts/ .github/` returns nothing. `scripts/novel_runner.sh` selects a phase by walking `find workspace -name PROMPT.md` in `sort` order and taking the first directory with no `.done`, no `.blocked` and no `.retired` marker, and it writes `.done` itself when a phase completes. Nothing in the dispatcher consults the ledger, and the ledger's being three volumes stale has never once changed which phase ran.** The file is controller-owned, this project has never edited it, and this phase may not, and it should still be repaired by whoever owns it, because a file that says the project is at Chapter 11 is a trap for the next reader even where it is not a trap for the machine. **And the thing that is real, in place of the thing that was not, is this: `workspace/volume-04/repair-0001/` had no `.done` marker when this block was written, and `workspace/volume-05/plan-0001/` is the next directory in `sort` order, so the repair phase would be selected again ahead of it and the Volume 05 prompt would be unreachable. The marker is the controller's — `scripts/novel_runner.sh` touches it on completion and this phase may not — and it is the controller's to write when this phase finishes. It is written down here because if that completion step does not run, this line is the only place that says why the book stopped, and a volume-audit gate and a repair phase are both later than a stale ledger in the things that cost this project time.**

**1a. AND THE COUNTS ABOVE ARE TWO COUNTS AND NOT ONE, because *twenty batches* and *nineteen batch phases* are both true and the previous sentence printed only the first and let it be read as a phase count.** The book has twenty batches of chapters: Volume 01 holds Chapters 1–50 in five, Volume 02 holds 51–100 in five, Volume 03 holds 101–150 in five and Volume 04 holds 151–200 in five. It has **nineteen** `workspace/<volume>/batch-*/PROMPT.md` directories, because **Volume 04's first batch has no directory: Chapters 151–160 were written under phase id `next`** — `state/current.md`'s own Batch 0001 block says so at its last line, *Current phase: Volume 04 Batch 0001 complete (151–160)*, and the prompt it created was `workspace/volume-04/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`. **A pass told to count batches must say which of the two it is counting, and this is the eighth time in this project that a bare count has meant two things at once.** Two further counts in the same family, measured in the same minute: **there are three volume-close phase directories, for Volumes 02, 03 and 04, and not four; Volume 01 has none, and no `## CLOSED` block in `outline/volume-01.md`, and this project has never run a volume audit for it either. Volume 01 is treated as canon and closed throughout these state files and that is not what this note disputes — what is disputed is a count of four close phases, which nothing in the tree supports.** And **twenty batch reviews are still the right expectation, because the expectation is about batches of chapters and not about directories**; seven exist and thirteen are missing, and the thirteen does not move because one of the twenty has no directory.


**2. THE REVIEW RECORD IS A GAP IN THREE OF THE FOUR VOLUMES, NOT IN ONE, AND IT IS A REAL GAP. Measured, not read out of a list:** `reviews/volume-01/` holds three batch reviews out of five and **no volume audit at all**; `reviews/volume-02/` holds three out of five and has an audit; **`reviews/volume-03/` holds `batch-0001.md` only, and no volume audit**; `reviews/volume-04/` held nothing until the audit this block answers. **That is **thirteen missing batch reviews and two missing volume audits, and the volume-audit gate has been skipped at least twice, and this repair phase exists because it was.** **The audit and the repair prompt both said twelve missing batch reviews and that figure is one short, and it is the seventh time a count in this project has been read or estimated instead of measured, which is the mechanism and not the accident: the four volumes have five batches each and twenty batch reviews are expected, and seven exist — Volume 01 holds three of five, Volume 02 three of five, Volume 03 one of five, Volume 04 none — and 2 + 2 + 4 + 5 is thirteen. Print what the directory holds.** **Volume 01 is the more valuable of the two missing audits and it is not the one the audit named:** it is the volume that established the device canon — the leg, the counting, the refusal of comfort — that the Volume 04 audit has just found misrecorded, and nothing has ever measured it against its chapters. **Nothing here is repaired and nothing is papered over with a document written in a repair pass's voice about work a repair pass did not witness. Both are open items in the hand-off, each wants its own phase with its own prompt, against `outline/volume-01.md` and against the `## CLOSED` block of `outline/volume-03.md` and the chapters, and both are left open. The Volume 03 verdict may change what Volume 05 inherits, which is exactly why it must be a real audit and not a paragraph.**

### THE HAND-OFF IS UNCHANGED, AND THIS BLOCK RESOLVES NOTHING

**Every open thread in `state/open-threads.md`'s top block is picked up whole and is still whole.** The new question is unanswered and is not compressed into one word. The carrying is still off nothing. The blank in the third line is a blank. Nesh Arrad's nine months has twenty-one days gone of it and the letter has not arrived and may not be said to have arrived. **A repair pass that resolves a thread is a second reversal and the outline forbids it, and this block repairs five lines of arithmetic and one blind instrument and nothing else.**

### A REVIEW OF THE REPAIR PHASE RAN, AND THIS IS ITS RECEIPT, 26 SEPTEMBER 2026, and it changed no chapter and no planned plot and no verdict

**It read this block, the fifty chapter files and the live blocks of the six state files, and it re-ran this block's instruments itself rather than against any figure printed here. It confirmed all five repairs verbatim at their cited lines, the arithmetic behind R2 and R3 against the volume's own chapter-and-day table, the four unchanged line counts, the day-phrase table, the case-sensitive pair, the zero weekday scan, the zero month-date count, the fifty-four month-word hits, the twenty-one bold lines and their exact eleven, the five panels, the five duplicates at seventy characters, the twenty-one-row day-count table against every chapter's Shelf day, all eight leg citations and `190:88` being empty, and all seventeen offset claims then standing. It found eight things wrong in the record and none of them in the prose, and all eight are corrected above, in this file's siblings, and in the Volume 05 prompt.**

**1. R4 IS CONFIRMED, AND THE CONFIRMATION IS RECORDED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, because the repair phase filed it as a mechanical decision and a reviewer is entitled to look again.** Recounting `200:51` as three things — a lease nobody has renewed and nobody has refused, a sheet with a blank in the third line now in a book six miles up a road that goes north, and a register about nine inches thick that is the sixth thing on a nail — is arithmetically sound, because those are the three the sentence names and the old count of five did not match them. **What the recount costs is the phrase *about seven thousand loads in it and one pair of hands*, which stood in the same sentence before the repair and no longer stands in the count, and a reader may reasonably want that hand back.** It is confirmed, for the reason the repair phase gave and because the declined alternative is worse: naming the ninety-one numbered sheets in the yard and the six things on the nail would add two objects to the volume's last image and change the rhythm of a paragraph whose rhythm is the point of it. **The one thing that is not settled and cannot be settled by a fixer is whether the image is worth the loss, and the honest form of that is to say the loss out loud, which is what this line is.** The prose stands. No later pass may reopen `200:51` on this ground without a writer's reason and not a count.

**2. THE THREE FIGURES THAT WERE PRINTED AS ONE. The eleven emphasis lines are all in Batch 0001, at Chapters 151–160, and not spread across the first two batches as the twelve-findings banner and FINDING 8 both said.** The whole of the bold damage is in the first ten chapters. **3. The duplicate count was mixing its unit.** Five at seventy characters is five lines and five occurrences and the units agree; ten at forty is ten lines and seventeen occurrences and they do not, because one of the ten stands at nine sites. **4. `186:87` was called a negative instance of the leg in the same sentence that counts it among twenty lines that carry the strong form, and the line carries the strong form.** **5. The words *two hundred* are in Chapter 158 six times on five lines and not five times.** **6. Three of the seven file offsets were not printed, and `state/index.md` quoted four `state/open-threads.md` line numbers that the +2 offset had already moved.** **7. The dispatch mechanism was misattributed in four files and in the Volume 05 prompt: nothing reads `state/phase-ledger.json`, and the real risk is an unclosed marker on the repair phase's own directory.** **8. *Twenty batches* and *nineteen batch phases* are both true, and *four volume closes* is true of neither: there are three close phase directories and Volume 01 has none.**

**WHAT THIS REVIEW DID NOT DO, and the list is the point of the receipt.** It did not edit a chapter, and it did not need to: every one of the eight findings was against a record, and the fifth repair this project made to prose five weeks ago stands as made. It did not resolve a thread, advance a device, answer the new question, restore the right of refusal, name a new enemy, or enlarge the world. It did not re-run the volume audit and does not claim to. **It confirmed the carried defects rather than clearing them: `160:96`, `167:41`, `158:77` and `158:79`, `174:53`, `173:55`, the block-quoted word at `167:9` and the eleven emphasis lines are all still in the prose and all still barred from being built on, and the outline's own £462 − £460 is still wrong.** And it left the two open items where it found them: the missing Volume 01 and Volume 03 audits are still missing, thirteen batch reviews are still missing, and nothing in this receipt stands in for either.

**ONE THING A LATER PASS SHOULD CARRY OUT OF THIS, and it is a method and not a fact: the eight findings were all found by measuring the tree — running the grep, listing the directory, counting the occurrences, reading the line — and not one of them by reading a list. Two of them, the ledger and the batch count, had been carried in four files each for a phase, and one of the two was a sentence about a machine this project has never read. The mechanism is always the same one and it is always cheap to check.**


### 1. THE MAP

**The Shelf's calendar is the grain year: fifty-two weeks, with bells inside a day, and the sixth bell as the turn only where a water tide is involved, which is at the ninth bridge and at Halloway and nowhere in Ryefold. Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. The bands, re-checked in a script for this record:** the fortieth is nought to six, the forty-first seven to thirteen, the forty-second fourteen to twenty, the forty-third twenty-one to twenty-seven, the forty-fourth twenty-eight to thirty-four, the forty-fifth thirty-five to forty-one, the forty-sixth forty-two to forty-eight, the forty-seventh forty-nine to fifty-five, the forty-eighth fifty-six to sixty-two, the forty-ninth sixty-three to sixty-nine, the fiftieth seventy to seventy-six.

**The fifty chapters, one row each. Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week and the run is Shelf days one to seventy.**

**TEN DAYS OF THE RUN CARRY NO CHAPTER, and three of them are single days that fall between two chapters and that no table in this project has ever printed. Seven of them are the forty-fourth week, days twenty-eight to thirty-four, which the outline records as unstated and unnarrated and which three chapters refer to without occupying (160:3, 163:103, 173:3). The other three are day forty-one, the seventh day of the forty-fifth week, between 170 and 171; day forty-four, the third day of the forty-sixth week, between 173 and 174; and day sixty-one, the sixth day of the forty-eighth week, between 190 and 191. A later pass should decide whether a day with no chapter is a hole to be printed or a thing the year does, and it should decide it before Volume 05 opens on day seventy-one, because the fiftieth week runs to seventy-six and there are six more days of this grain year after Chapter 200 and a close may say so and may not narrate them.**

**FINDING 1. THE DAY-COUNT. THE MANUSCRIPT CARRIES TWENTY-TWO INSTANCES, AND THIS SENTENCE SAID TWENTY-ONE UNTIL THE VOLUME 04 AUDIT, AND EVERY STATE FILE IN THIS PROJECT RECORDED ELEVEN OR TEN. The count is twenty-two and it is a count of instances. **The twenty-second is `156:85`, *Fifteenth day.*, a page heading in his own hand on a chapter whose Shelf day is fifteen, and it is the seventh time a list in this project has been short. **The twenty-one-row table below is deliberately not extended with it, because a row added in this block moves every line number below it and this project cites `state/current.md` by line - including its own §8 items, which the audit quotes by number.** **Note for a later pass that the record is three ways wrong and not two: this heading said twenty-one, `state/index.md` and `state/open-threads.md` said twenty-one, and this same file's FINDING 15, ninety lines below, still says the list is recorded as ten and the manuscript carries eleven. The archive blocks are superseded from the top of `state/continuity.md` and are not rewritten. See the audit at `reviews/volume-04/volume-audit.md` §6.1 and §12.3.**The device is the volume's own: he counts the days since he came off a boat, the way a man states a tide count, in his own page, in his own mouth, in the narration, or in another person's mouth naming it. The instrument is a scan for the number-word forms and a comparison of each figure against the chapter's Shelf day, and it was run over Chapters 151–200 for this record. Every instance below agrees with the day table in section 1, which is the whole of the finding: the prose is right and the list was short, and this is the sixth time in this project that a list has been short, and the first time it has been short by ten.**
